# Module — Tests: the scenario harness and its flow catalogue

> Status: **created 2026-10-12 with the scouts' scenario (E1 S3 of [PLAN_scout_model_calibration.md](PLAN_scout_model_calibration.md)); the
> private-task scenario and the `key` / `hygiene` rows added 2026-10-13 (E2 S4); the `access-check` row 2026-10-13 (E2 S5); the `tasks`,
> `access-check` and `hygiene` rows widened by the E2 code round, 2026-10-13.**
> The record the shared rule `common.scenario-tests` requires in every repository: where the harness is, how it runs, every flow with
> `covered` / `not covered` and a reason, what it does not prove, and when it runs. Adopted the way that rule's *Adoption* section asks — the
> catalogue first, most flows `not covered` on day one, closed flow by flow.
>
> **The minigit stack bench's Node scenarios (E2.S1, E2.S2) were added 2026-10-30** — see *The minigit stack bench scenarios* below; the C# catalogue
> test ignores that section.

## Where the harness is

In this repository, beside the unit tests: the scenario classes live in `tests/Bench.Tests/Cli/` (C#, the product's own language, holding the
product's own types and entry points). A scenario drives a verb exactly as `bench …` parses it — `ScoutsCommand.RunAsync(CommandLine.Parse([...]))`
or `Program.Run(args)` — against a **real Postgres** (Testcontainers `postgres:17`, `PostgresFixture`), **real git** repositories and real
history-free trees, and the real artefact root on disk. The only doubles are the collaborators a scenario cannot have: the paid CLIs, replaced
by `tests/FakeCli` (`fake-cli`), which replays recorded claude streams by the SHA-256 of their prompt and plays a codex assessor answering
recorded verdicts; the fake's home is the test's owned folder. Nothing in a scenario substitutes the subject.

## How it is run

The same command CI runs (`.github/workflows/ci.yml`), the test project's executable — never `dotnet test`:

```bash
dotnet build dew_flow_benchmark.slnx -c Release -m:4
./tests/Bench.Tests/bin/Release/net10.0/Bench.Tests.exe                                   # everything, scenarios included
./tests/Bench.Tests/bin/Release/net10.0/Bench.Tests.exe --filter-class "*ScoutsScenarioTests"   # the scouts scenario alone
```

Docker must be up (Testcontainers). The scenario needs no network and no paid model.

## When it runs

On every push and pull request, in CI, as part of the one test executable — no flow here needs a GPU, a paid model or a deployed target. A
flow that would (a real `claude` or `codex`) is exercised by its plan's pilot instead, named below.

**Run it alone on a workstation.** Measured 2026-10-12: two copies of the scout CLI classes started at once on one machine (CPU at 100 %, both
starting their Postgres containers, the suite at BelowNormal priority per the local CPU budget) failed three of 52 tests, every one in a
fixture's git call (`git init`, `git commit`, `git rev-parse`) reaching `DatedGitRepo`'s 30 s wall while the containers started; alone, the
same classes and the whole suite pass. No state is shared between those tests (each owns its repository, its trees, its fake CLI home and its
database), so the failure is the machine's contention, not the code's — and widening the wall would only hide the next such run.

## The flow catalogue

The list is **derived, not typed**: `ModuleTestsCatalogTests` reads the CLI's verbs out of `hosts/Cli/Program.cs`'s dispatch, the scouts'
sub-verbs out of `hosts/Cli/ScoutsCommand.cs`, and every route out of `src/Bench.Api/*.cs`, and fails when a flow is missing here, when a row
names a flow that no longer exists, or when a row marked `covered` names a test class the test assembly does not hold.

### CLI — `bench <verb>`

| flow | status | test | reason |
|---|---|---|---|
| `bench plan` | not covered | — | no scenario catalogued yet; `PlanCommand` is unit-tested in `tests/Bench.Tests/Cli` |
| `bench run` | not covered | — | `RunCommandTests` / `RunRetrievalTests` drive it against fakes; not yet reviewed against the scenario rule |
| `bench resume` | not covered | — | `ResumeCommandTests` exist; not yet reviewed against the scenario rule |
| `bench judge` | not covered | — | no scenario catalogued yet |
| `bench solve` | not covered | — | `SolveCommandTests` exist; not yet reviewed against the scenario rule |
| `bench report` | not covered | — | `ReportCommandTests` exist; not yet reviewed against the scenario rule |
| `bench sweep` | not covered | — | `SweepRecoveryTests` exist; not yet reviewed against the scenario rule |
| `bench prune` | not covered | — | no scenario catalogued yet |
| `bench rescore` | not covered | — | no scenario catalogued yet |
| `bench telemetry` | not covered | — | `TelemetryCommandTests` exist; not yet reviewed against the scenario rule |
| `bench sessions` | not covered | — | no scenario catalogued yet; the hook and the collector are separate hosts |
| `bench variants` | not covered | — | `VariantsCommandTests` exist; not yet reviewed against the scenario rule |
| `bench lanes` | not covered | — | no scenario catalogued yet |
| `bench questions` | not covered | — | `QuestionsCommandTests` exist; not yet reviewed against the scenario rule |
| `bench models` | not covered | — | `ModelsCommandTests` exist; not yet reviewed against the scenario rule |
| `bench gate` | not covered | — | the gate's driver tests (`GateDriverCommandTests`) drive a fake coai; not yet reviewed against the scenario rule |
| `bench probes` | not covered | — | `ProbesDriverCommandTests` drive the verbs against the fake CLI and a real store; not yet reviewed against the scenario rule |
| `bench scouts` | covered | `ScoutsScenarioTests` | the sub-verbs below |
| `bench version` | not covered | — | prints a constant; nothing to drive end to end |
| `bench help` | not covered | — | prints the usage text; `CliContractTests` reads it, no flow behind it |

The scouts have no route yet: the read API and the console's Scouts tab are E3 S8 (plan decision D8), which extends `ScoutsScenarioTests` so the
API's bytes equal the CLI report's.

## The scouts scenario (`ScoutsScenarioTests`)

`bench scouts run` over two tasks (t01, t02 of one local repository; |G| = 3) × the arms A1 and B1, the real store and real history-free trees,
the fake claude replaying a recorded stream per (task, arm). The harness crashes once the first cell's `result` line reached disk (a dead
owner, as a killed bench leaves); `status` shows the claimed cell; `resume` settles that attempt once from its stream and re-runs the cell; the
cell (t02, B1) goes VOID — its driver launched the scouts on the wrong model. Then `score`, `assess` (the fake codex calls a claim wrong when it
names `README.md` or `src/Beta.cs`), `report --json`, `report`, `prune`. The test carries the hand computation as a comment table and asserts
the report against it: the verdict label (`preliminary`), W1's intervals (one repository → not estimable), each arm's recall, wrong claims per
10, team time, team cost, cost per right file, caller load, the void count, the claim counts, and the ledger identity over five attempts.

## The private-task scenario (`ScoutsPrivateTaskTests`, E2 S4)

ONE synthetic private task (`p03`, language PHP, in a local `file://` repository) whose statement, three G paths, an identifier the diff added, a
claim and the repository's url carry planted strings that are on NO deny-list — the scrubber would not catch them, only the projection does.
Since S7 the repository lists five private candidates on that change (a repository needs five to be drawn), their PR numbers assigned by hash
order under the seed so `p03` is the main task and `p01` its reserve whatever the clone's folder is called.
It is driven through `tasks` (with the campaign key, the campaign string and the private tasks file), `run` (the fake claude, its KEY FILES and
CLAIMS carrying the plants), `status`, `score --handcheck` (the public file and the local companion), `assess` (the fake codex), `report`,
`report --json`, the `cells.jsonl` and RESULTS writers, the public export walk and the log sink; then through every error path — a malformed
private candidate, a missing mirror, an I/O failure injected into the tree (a locked file) and into the artefact root, and the unhandled
exception those raise through `Program.Run`. No planted string reaches stdout, stderr, a public file, an export row or the process's log file;
the local-only files (the private tasks file, the companion, the withheld detail, the fault file) do carry them, which is how the test knows its
plants are alive. A third test takes the key through its lifecycle: `key init` refused inside a checkout and a second time; a verb without the
key refused (4) with nothing created; an edited statement, a wrong key and the public file alone each refused by label.

## The minigit stack bench scenarios (E2.S1 and E2.S2, `spikes/minigit-bench/`)

A second harness lives in this repository: the Node test suite of the minigit stack bench's agent sandbox, in `spikes/minigit-bench/test/` (and, for
the process fixes of E2.S1 that live in the sibling spike, `spikes/policy-ab-quick/test/`). It is written in the spike's own language (ES modules, Node
26) and drives the real instruments: a real `bwrap` box, a real egress proxy and credential broker on real unix sockets, a real session launch around
a stub `claude`, and the committed fixture of one real paid session. What a scenario substitutes is only what it cannot have: the paid API (a local
stub upstream replays a recorded SSE body), the real token (a FAKE `sk-ant-fake-…` value), and the CLI (a script that does what the real one does at
its edges). **The flow list is derived**: the Node test `module-tests-catalog.test.mjs` reads the verdict ids out of `SMOKE_ASSUMPTIONS`
(`lib/smoke-verdicts.mjs`) and every `*.test.mjs` file of the spike's `test/` directory, and fails when one is not named in this section, or when a
row names a test file that does not exist. The design is [module_minigit_bench.md](module_minigit_bench.md).

**How it is run** (from the repository root; ~30 minutes at the lowered priority the local CPU budget requires; **0 failed and 0 skipped** is the bar):

```bash
BENCH_REQUIRE_SANDBOX=1 node spikes/minigit-bench/run-tests.mjs                          # everything in spikes/minigit-bench/test
node spikes/minigit-bench/run-tests.mjs test/broker.test.mjs test/egress.test.mjs        # a few files (globs are relative to the spike)
node spikes/minigit-bench/run-tests.mjs '../policy-ab-quick/test/proc.test.mjs' '../policy-ab-quick/test/audit.test.mjs'   # the E2.S1 process fixes
```

`BENCH_REQUIRE_SANDBOX=1` turns a test that skips because the host lacks something (`bwrap`, user namespaces, the network, the token file) into a
failure, so "0 skipped" is a claim about this machine and not about a gap. **When it runs:** on this machine only (it needs WSL2 with `bwrap`; CI does not
run it), by whoever changes the spike, before every commit that touches it and in full before a review gate; the paid re-smoke (one session, cap $0.20)
runs by the operator's word only, once per code round, and the fixture it produces is then re-checked by `smoke-real.test.mjs` on every run.

**At its start the runner sweeps (E6.S6).** `run-tests.mjs` calls the conventions' own `sweepLeftovers` (`.agents/conventions/tools/lib/leftovers-sweep.mjs`) with this repository's token `bench`, in the shape of `spikes/lang-bench/run-tests.mjs`: what a KILLED earlier run of any `bench` spike's tests left is retired before the tests start, on the rule's two keys: an owner-form folder in the temp root and an owner-form git worktree of this repository, each whose claim names a dead process and which is older than ten minutes. The legacy-named folders of the shared `test/helpers.mjs` and `test/fake-home.mjs` makers (`testsToDelete-mgb-…`, F2's) are class `other` for the token `bench`: the sweep neither lists nor touches them, and they wait for the followups plan's S2. One stderr line says how many were removed and kept, and one line per kept path follows it with its reason (`young`, `unmarked`, a live owner…); a sweep that throws is one stderr line and never changes the exit code. The policy-ab-quick tests run through this runner (the third command above), so they get the sweep; the conventions' `low-priority.mjs node --test` way that `todo/PLAN_minigit_stack_bench_E2.md:86-87` names does not sweep.

| `B-S2` | covered | `test/smoke-real.test.mjs`, `test/broker-body.test.mjs` |
| `B-S3` | covered | `test/smoke-real.test.mjs`, `test/session-scan.test.mjs`, `test/link-class.test.mjs` |
| `B-S3b` | not covered | retired by design: the models refuse the in-box probe; B-S3's positive control covers the CLI's environ from the host |
| `B-$1` | covered | `test/smoke-real.test.mjs`, `test/budget.test.mjs`, `test/crosscheck.test.mjs` |
| `B-O1`, `B-T1`, `B-E1`, `B-A1`, `B-A2`, `B-A3`, `B-H1`, `B-C1` | covered | `test/smoke-real.test.mjs` |
| `B-K1` | covered | `test/smoke-real.test.mjs`, `test/janitor.test.mjs` |
| `B-K2` | covered | `test/smoke-k2.test.mjs`; not exercised by the committed fixture (the session ended by itself) |
| Bench PR 4a, S7: the v1 suite seals to the same bytes as before (the pack's json pinned by a committed hash; a blob sealed before the widening, with an invented fixture key, still unseals to the same tree; `sealSuite`'s four outputs; the container's constants), and `readKey` takes a caller's remedy | covered | `minigit-bench/test/heldout-seal.test.mjs`; `test/fixtures/heldout-seal/` |

**What this harness does NOT prove.**

- **That the real API behaves as the stub upstream does.** The broker's tests replay one recorded SSE body and scripted error answers; the real
  API is only seen in a paid smoke, once per code round. Error shapes, a stream cut at a given event, billing of a reset request: assumed, not measured.
- **Anything about a CLI other than 2.1.310** (the read-back pins the version) or a model the price table does not hold (the broker charges the dearest rates and says so).
- **That `bwrap` isolation holds on another kernel.** The box tests run against this machine's kernel and `bwrap`; a different host is a new measurement.
- **The dotnet hide list beyond this host's layout** (amendment D-9). `box-dotnet-paths.test.mjs` shows each path SENDS the tmpfs over an invented listing; that the tmpfs hides an SDK is observed only in `agent-box-dotnet.test.mjs`, on this machine's tree (`/usr/lib/dotnet`, SDK 10.0.114 beside 11.0.100, muxer and `host/fxr` 11.0.0). Not hidden and not tested as hidden: `host/fxr`, the files `library-packs/*.nupkg`, `metadata/workloads/<band>`. Not provided at all: a stable view against a dotnet version installed WHILE a box runs (the list is taken when the box is built; `module_minigit_bench.md` names the limit). The scan of `box-builders-scan.test.mjs` reads the two spikes only, not `spikes/lang-bench`, and recognises the shapes it names: an argv assembled from strings it does not know as bwrap's is not seen. The scoring box takes the same list and is observed by one probe in the real box (the SDKs it lists, and the SDK a folder with no `global.json` is given); the tests added for the amendment build no tree in it. And `preflight.test.mjs` uses the `claude` on PATH: when that is not the pinned CLI the test is red for the CLI, whatever the SDKs are (the state of this host on 2026-11-19: 2.1.328 on PATH, pin 2.1.310).
- **The TLS edge.** The SNI gate is tested against recorded real hellos and a local upstream; what a CDN edge does with a second hello, a renegotiation or an
  HTTP/2 `:authority` is not provided by the proxy (residual risk, written in `module_minigit_bench.md`).
- **Effect tests that need the network** (`egress-box.test.mjs`, the npm and dotnet restores) run against real hosts: they say so and, without
  `BENCH_REQUIRE_SANDBOX=1`, skip when the host has none.
- **That two test files never share a cell id.** `envFor` of `test/box-helpers.mjs` names a box's cell `cell-<rid>`, and the rid is a per-file counter (`s1`, `s2`, …), so boxes of different files running side by side carry the same `BENCH_CELL`. A test that COUNTS or ENDS the processes of a cell must draw an id of its own (the janitor's tests and `build-servers.test.mjs` do); nothing checks that a new one does.
- **One real session.** The committed fixture is one paid run on one day; it proves the verdict rules read a real stream, not that every session behaves so.
- **Concurrency beyond a few requests**, and a run of days: the broker's budget and the janitor are exercised at the scale of seconds.

## The language-comparison runner scenarios (E1.S8a parts i and ii-a, `spikes/lang-bench/`)

The third harness here is the Node suite of the language-comparison bench (`node spikes/lang-bench/run-tests.mjs`, lowered priority, at most
`min(8, CPUs - 1)` workers). Node test files start the Python units of `test/py/` under `python3 -I -B` in an owned `TMPDIR`
(`test/support/py-units.mjs`) and register one Node test per Python test, so every guarantee appears under its own name. The design is
[module_lang_bench.md](module_lang_bench.md). This is **part i**: the lexer, both parsers, the matcher, the olympiad path, the report, the
command line, the pin. **Part ii-a** (the execution layer's options for the olympiad path: launch prefix and status, the case clock, the pidfd waits, the cgroup, the case view,
V11 and V12) adds the rows marked ii-a. Part ii-b (networking, the daemon and web runs, signals) adds its own rows.

**The units' sandbox (E6.S6; the machine's rule: Python only in a sandbox).** The `.py` units run without the host's interpreter in a `wslc` container, `python:3.14-slim@sha256:9aaa474097d094040649f2162a627c0049b408f52138d980916788d80f04802f` (Python 3.14.10): `wslc.exe run --rm --name <owned name> --label testsToDelete.owner=<owner record of the launching shell> --cpus 2 -m 2G -u <uid>:<gid> -e TMPDIR=/tmp -v '\\wsl.localhost\Ubuntu\<repo>\spikes\lang-bench:/w:ro' -w /w python:3.14-slim@sha256:… sh -c 'python3 -I -B -m unittest discover -v -s test/py -p "test_*.py" & wait $!'`. The name comes from the helper (`ownedName('pyunits')`), the label is the owner record of the launching shell (not a literal), the spike is mounted READ-ONLY, `sh` is PID 1 so orphans are reaped (a bare `python3` as PID 1 fails the three group-kill units), `TMPDIR=/tmp` is the container's own and dies with `--rm`. It runs 814 of the 829 units; the other 15 (`test_rs_scope.py`, `test_rs_owner.py`, `test_rs_interrupt.py`, the users of `box_support.Scope`) need the host's user systemd manager and `bwrap` and skip there; they run in an owned throwaway venv (`python3 -I -m venv --without-pip <owned folder>/v`, then `BENCH_REQUIRE_SANDBOX=1 TMPDIR=<owned folder> <venv>/bin/python3 -I -B -m unittest discover -v -s test/py -p test_rs_scope.py`, likewise the other two: 31, 27 and 3 units), removed by exact path afterwards. **Not covered by that sandbox:** the WHOLE `node spikes/lang-bench/run-tests.mjs` suite, whose execution layer finds `python3` on its fixed `SBIN_PATH` (`lib/exec-layer/run.mjs`), so it runs the system interpreter whatever `PATH` holds; it runs that way only where the owner allowed it (standard library, `-I -B`, nothing installed, lowered priority, one run at a time).

| ii-a (round 5): a cgroup outside the cgroup2 mount that holds a controllers file is refused and untouched; mountinfo parsing (a v1 mount is no root, escaped mount points); a box in a descendant, the parent or a neighbour is a mismatch, and the reason names both cgroups; `populated 1` makes an existing cgroup busy and `populated 0` frees it; the box's pidfd is opened before it is judged and stopped through; the fallback kill goes through re-checked pidfds; the plain path gives unknown memory for a cgroup the launcher is not in (a real foreign cgroup); a never-existing cgroup is exit 3; an extra counts for `fork` only after 100 ms (controlled clock; the reviewer's run.sh under bounded CPU burners: at most 2 false forks in 20 rounds, the documented 1-in-160 rate, and a broken rule is red); the orphan rule by pidfd; the group wait scans /proc at a 1 to 20 ms pace; the stop handler blocks the signals first and two back-to-back signals run one teardown; the swallowed problem leaves a note; no stopping signal stays blocked after the handler; the fallback kill reads the listing once per batch of 64 and survives EMFILE or ENFILE without a descriptor left open; a malformed mountinfo line is skipped; a directory under no cgroup2 mount (or a cgroup2 mounted as a subtree) is refused with that reason; no runner line over 160 characters | covered | `test/runner-owner.test.mjs`, `test/runner-cgroup.test.mjs`, `test/runner-track.test.mjs`, `test/runner-kill.test.mjs`, `test/runner-scope.test.mjs`, `test/runner-shape.test.mjs` |
| ii-a: the layer's options on the command line (exit 4: a cgroup with a prefix and no status, a template without `{case}` for several cases, a wrapper file without a cgroup, the reserved id `empty-program`; exit 3: a host without `pidfd_open`, below Linux 5.14 or without cgroup v2, a cgroup that is not this case's; SIGHUP like SIGTERM) and the whole command line through a real scope with bwrap's real status lines, and SIGTERM mid-case | covered | `test/runner-layer-options.test.mjs`, `test/runner-interrupt.test.mjs` |
| Running a daemon or web scenario (processes, sockets, signals, readiness, V3, V5, V7, `overflow`) | not covered | part ii-b |

**Round 4 and what it does not prove either.** A real scope is removed by the system when its last process exits (the tests keep it with a holder named as the wrapper); `memory` of a quick
program can be unknown when the box is gone before the membership look. The wrong-cgroup proof is on real cgroups under a delegated user scope; the fake cgroup folders of the other tests are
let through by `Workspace.run` (they are no cgroup2 node and no box is in them) unless a test says `real=True`, and the counters a fake cgroup ends with are written by the program. The timing tests (the
printer's ratio, the wall bounds) have wide margins and were run under CPU load; a failure under load is re-run alone.

**What this part does not prove.** Memory is never judged without a cgroup (every case says `not certified`); with one, `memory.peak` and `memory.events` are what the
kernel reports for the directory named, not that the layer's limit is the right one (that is E1.S8b's probe). A plain transient scope is removed by the system when its last process exits, so a clean case's `memory.peak` can be gone before it is read (the record then says unknown with its reason; E1.S8b's scope holder keeps it). The scope path is proven on a plain delegated user scope behind
bwrap, not on E1.S8b's layer (its `prog/` leaf, `scope-entry.sh`, `nsenter` and a private network namespace do not exist yet), and the wrapper exclusion is proven only through a file the test writes. The
status lines are proven from bwrap 0.11's real output and from shell launchers; `exit-code` for a program killed by a signal is whatever bwrap writes. The runner does not
signal the program in this part (an olympiad program is only waited for and killed), so B1's pid choice is proven for the wait and the record, not for a `TERM`. An interrupt is proven by SIGTERM and SIGHUP on the plain path, by SIGTERM on a real scope, and for the spawn window by the registry (a signal landing on the exact
instruction between the Popen and its name is not provoked: the deferred mask and the registry are what close it). `fork` is observed at a 20 ms cadence: a stray process that lives
less than two looks and is gone before the leftover read is never seen. The three pilot specs' samples do not exist yet (E1.S6), so the runner has only run
FORMAT's own examples and toys; a behaviour beyond the scenarios written here is not proven.

## The execution layer scenarios (E1.S8b, `spikes/lang-bench/`)

The layer (`lib/exec-layer/`), its probe and its memory policy are proven on the real host, under `BENCH_REQUIRE_SANDBOX=1` with nothing skipped: real bwrap, real user scopes, a real holder, the real
runner. Each test that names a mechanism builds the wrong one beside the right one, so the defect has a shape. Design: [module_lang_bench.md](module_lang_bench.md).

| The box hides the host: no home, no `/mnt`, no `/run`, no user bus, no `cmd.exe`; `bash -c true` is silent; `de_DE.utf8` is listed; the bind scan finds a broad bind | covered | `test/exec-layer-isolation.test.mjs` |
| The shared network namespace (the driver reaches the program's port; `--unshare-net` would refuse it), the holder's `lo`-only namespace and bound, the capability drop, T-L33 for a Python, a Node and a C# stub, the exec half of T-L31 | covered | `test/exec-layer-netns.test.mjs` |

**Observed (2026-11-05, branch `fix/lang-v2-marker-test-race`, §14 A1 step 0):** `The_marker_kill_hook_really_kills_a_hanging_child_before_its_folder_is_removed`, alone, 14 busy loops on 14 CPUs: before, 108 of 120 runs passed (9 failed at the `after` hook's scan, the race of E1.S3's `s3a-j1.txt:1956` and `s3a-j2.txt:1944`; 3 failed in the body, the scan that sees the pid before the child's exec and not during it); after, 150 of 150 and 150 of 150. The three `probe-scope-wait` cases were run red with the helper checking once (2 red), green, and red again with the wait out (2 red). Whole runs on the branch: `BENCH_REQUIRE_SANDBOX=1 LANG_V2_TASK_REPO=<task repository checkout> node spikes/lang-bench/run-tests.mjs` 1785 of 1785 (1782 plus the 3 new), 130 s, host load 6.2 at the start; `BENCH_REQUIRE_SANDBOX=1 node spikes/minigit-bench/run-tests.mjs` first run 1239 of 1240 (`A_cancel_during_the_quiet_wait_of_the_real_wiring_ends_it_at_once`, on the S5 list, host load 9.45 at the start; recorded in S5), the next whole run 1240 of 1240, 447 s, host load 3.4; `node --test spikes/lang-explore/test/` 302 of 303, 1 skipped by design, 173 s; `specs-record.mjs --check`, `pin-runner.mjs --check`, `pin-exec-layer.mjs --check` exit 0; `plan-lifecycle.mjs` clean; `push-check.mjs --self` PASS.

**What these do not prove.** The probe proves this host (WSL2, systemd 259, bwrap 0.11.1, kernel 6.18): a host without unprivileged user namespaces, with another `DefaultOOMPolicy` or without a
delegated user manager is exit 3, not a pass. T-L43's two toy programs show that bounded live data completes and that growing live data is `memory limit` in both stacks; they do not prove that a
task's real allocation pattern would, and the policy that decides OP-K is the team lead's. The sweep's 10-minute floor is proven by a parameter, not by waiting. `--cap-drop ALL` is proven for the
capability set and `lo`, not as a complete sandbox. The C# programs are built with no package; a task that needs the cache snapshots (E1.S4) is not covered by these builds. Timing-sensitive tests (the
holder's bound, the scope's end) have margins of seconds and were run under the full suite's load.

## The conformance checker scenarios (E1.S8, `spikes/lang-bench/`)

*Last full run, 2026-11-04, branch `feat/lang-v2-e1-s8` (batch 4 included): `BENCH_REQUIRE_SANDBOX=1 LANG_V2_TASK_REPO=<the task repository> node spikes/lang-bench/run-tests.mjs` 2134 of 2134; plain `node spikes/lang-bench/run-tests.mjs` 2129 tests, 2102 pass, 27 skipped (the cases that need the task repository), 0 fail; `BENCH_REQUIRE_SANDBOX=1 node spikes/minigit-bench/run-tests.mjs` 1240 of 1240; `node --test spikes/lang-explore/test/` (the exploratory pilot) 302 of 303, 1 skipped by design; all green on the first run, at a host load that fell from 35 to 5 during them. The earlier batches' timing flakes under load (`todo/PLAN_bench_test_leftovers_followups.md` S5) did not recur. Batch 4's own cases are in `conformance-batch4.test.mjs` (29 tests). The ten real drafts (read from their branches, not committed) give, after the batch, only F-STDERR, F-MEMORY, F-PROCS and F-ENV (they are FORMAT v1 drafts) and the missing samples; before it, drafts 04, 06 and 08 also had a false FAIL each.*

The checker of a spec against FORMAT (`node spikes/lang-bench/conformance.mjs`; design in [module_lang_bench.md](module_lang_bench.md), section *The conformance check (E1.S8)*). The tests run it in this process on
copies of three synthetic task repositories (`test/fixtures/conformance/<kind>/`, one conforming spec with its samples per kind, and a FORMAT stand-in with the real document's sections 1 and 2) in owned
folders; `edit` throws unless its needle is there exactly once, and `phrase` is the same for a sentence that wraps. Each negative case shows that the unchanged spec is `ok` for the check and that the planted
change gives the named `FAIL` line, so a checker that finds nothing (or everything) fails. The real FORMAT and the real spec 01 are read only when `LANG_V2_TASK_REPO` is set (skipped otherwise, a failure under
`BENCH_REQUIRE_SANDBOX=1`).

| Flow | Status | Test files |
|---|---|---|
| A conforming spec of each kind: one `ok` per check, exit 0; the command's streams; exit 4 for each usage and format error; olympiad spec under A's and B's FORMAT | covered | `conformance.test.mjs` (14) |
| Checks 1 to 6: one failing fixture per deviation (title, header, sections, amendments, ids, spans, sentences, slots, T and G), `The_checker_still_finds_a_known_deviation`, the exact `T/G` line | covered | `conformance-checks.test.mjs` (48) |
| Checks 7 to 10: folder against section 6, cells and blocks against the bytes, the runner's lines quoted, CP1's F11 forms, the env keys, links, plan ids, the needles file, the glyph, the reading block | covered | `conformance-samples.test.mjs` (32) |
| The runner: python or runner missing, a runner that ends in another way, a hang stopped at the ceiling with its process tree (a session of its own), an olympiad spec without either | covered | `conformance-runner.test.mjs` (8) |
| FORMAT is the source: a retyped list would pass an old spec, a one-word change is followed, a FORMAT missing a block, a slot, a placement, a table or a fact is exit 4, the real FORMAT's ids all found, spec 01 observed | covered | `conformance-sentences.test.mjs` (16) |
| The env lines against the layer's own lists, a retyped list missing a new key | covered | `conformance-env.test.mjs` (10) |
| The stack words of the series (`inputs/stack-words-lang.json`) found in a requirement and in a sample file, named by the word; a retyped list would miss a word added to the file; the real FORMAT and spec 01 hold none | covered | `conformance-stack-words.test.mjs` (37) |
| The review's fixes (batch 3): the fail-closed items C1, C2 and C11 (silent runner, the record and the reader's structure, slot phrases); the structure items C5 to C10, C12, C15, C16, C18 to C20; the text items C3, C4, C13, C14, C17; C22 | covered | `conformance-failclosed.test.mjs`, `conformance-batch3.test.mjs`, `conformance-batch3b.test.mjs`, `conformance-needles.test.mjs` |
| Batch 4, the narrowings N1 to N8 (a wrapped cross reference, a wrapping code span, a value in a span, ids that are words, index forms, the reading block's script-only words, the second F-TIMING, the record's gate state), each with the true deviation that still fails | covered | `conformance-batch4.test.mjs` (29) |
| The shape rule (under 50 lines, complexity at most 4, no exemption) on the conformance files, the FORMAT readers and the FORMAT tools, one test per file naming its offenders | covered | `code-shape-lang.test.mjs` (22) |

**Red again.** The production line of each guarantee was taken out in turn and the named tests watched red (ten mutations, two of them of `runBounded` and of the env list; record in `~/rsd/lang-v2/briefs/s8-reds.txt`).
The checker was written before its tests, so the first reds are the failures the tests showed on the way (a fingerprint that missed a changed word, a sentence outside every requirement), not a red of the whole checker.
**Spec 01 under the real FORMAT** (observation, not a requirement; E1.S2 re-conforms the spec): with an empty samples folder, `file`, `header`, `sections`, `r-ids`, `T/G`, `sample-forms`, `sample-env` and `no-needle` are ok; `sentences` names F-STDERR, F-MEMORY and F-ENV as altered and F-PROCS as missing; `samples` names the six sample files it does not find.

## The FORMAT tools scenarios (E1.S9, `spikes/lang-bench/`)

*Last full run, 2026-11-04, with E1.S4 and E1.S9 merged and the record at the gated FORMAT (`d293bafc…`): `BENCH_REQUIRE_SANDBOX=1 LANG_V2_TASK_REPO=<the task repository> node spikes/lang-bench/run-tests.mjs` 1782 of 1782, the FORMAT files with `specs-record` 338 of 338. The numbers below are kept as they were observed on the way.*

The bench's part of the FORMAT story: the grammar extractor, the class check (T-L56) and the bounds test (T-L58). Refusal cases use a synthetic FORMAT built in `test/support/format-fixtures.mjs`
(the shape of the real document, none of its content; `edit` throws unless its needle is there exactly once, so a case never edits nothing). The positive cases and `--self` also run on the real draft when
`LANG_V2_TASK_REPO` names the task repository checkout (after a check that its `todo/tasks/FORMAT.md` has the sha256 of the record's `A.sha256`); without the variable they skip, under
`BENCH_REQUIRE_SANDBOX=1` they throw. Design: [module_lang_bench.md](module_lang_bench.md), section *FORMAT v2 (E1.S9)*.

| Flow | Status | Test files |
|---|---|---|

**What these do not prove.** They do not prove that the real FORMAT's later versions keep the shape the readers expect (a changed sentence makes a reader fail loudly, which is the point); the definition rule for 2.1's rows
(label, else the introducing paragraph) was checked on the draft, where it frees exactly the 14 daemon and web sentences, not on any later text. The format's own gate and freeze are not run here.
## The cache snapshot scenarios (E1.S4, `spikes/lang-bench/`)

The package sets and the two cache snapshots are proven with the real thing: one real run of `make-snapshots.mjs` (network only through the egress proxy, in v1's agent box with S5's toolchains bound and the distribution's dotnet hidden), then fresh boxes that hold only the seeded snapshot and a proxy that refuses every registry. Each test that names a mechanism builds the wrong one beside the right one. Design: [module_lang_bench.md](module_lang_bench.md), section Package sets and snapshots. The network file runs only under `BENCH_REQUIRE_SANDBOX=1`.

**The registration (2026-11-04):** the team lead registered both snapshots with `make-snapshots.mjs` (C# `452c7a57…`, 60 packages; Node `bebdc7a2…`, 164 packages; the full hashes are in `module_lang_bench.md`). The tests above never touch `~/rsd/lang-v2/caches/` or `inputs/cache-snapshots-lang.json`: the real build runs in an owned folder with its own `--caches`, `--record` and `--gate-lock`. A fast test renders `PACKAGES.md` from the REGISTERED record and the recipe for both stacks and checks that the C# file names neither Node nor npm and the Node file names neither C#, NuGet nor PackageReference (the install line is per stack).

**Run numbers** (2026-11-04, one host, after the registration): `node spikes/lang-bench/run-tests.mjs`: 1449 tests, 1444 pass, 0 fail, 5 skipped (the five network cases, with their reason), 108 s. With `BENCH_REQUIRE_SANDBOX=1`: 1454 pass, 0 fail, 0 skipped, 154 s. `BENCH_REQUIRE_SANDBOX=1 node spikes/minigit-bench/run-tests.mjs`: 1240 pass, 0 fail, 0 skipped, 513 s. `node --test spikes/lang-explore/test/`: 303 tests, 302 pass, 0 fail, 1 skipped, 225 s. `plan-lifecycle.mjs`: clean. `push-check.mjs --self --branch feat/lang-v2-e1-s4 --repo .`: PASS. `sweeper-scan.mjs`: the same 7 owner-blind lines as `main`, none in a file of this story. The `test_rs_scope.py` fork test passed. Registered snapshots: C# 174,187,832 bytes and 60 packages; Node 180,132,320 bytes and 164 packages.

**What these do not prove.** One host (WSL2, bwrap 0.11.1, the egress proxy on a unix socket); the registries as they were on the build day (the x-ranges resolve to the highest version of their line that day, and the npm snapshot is not byte-reproducible: every npm index entry holds a time, so each build gave another tree hash while the manifest was identical; the registered Node folder is therefore irreplaceable and is backed up and verified by hash after registration, and the C# tree hash was identical in every build observed); a package an agent names without a version can still fail `ENOTCACHED` offline, because npm resolves the cached packument's `latest`; the snapshots were tested with the offered set and the three C# project shapes, not with every project an agent may write (settings such as `PublishAot` need packages that are not in the set, and NuGet audit warnings may become errors under `TreatWarningsAsErrors`); the gate lock's liveness is a pid probe, not a heartbeat.

## The workspace template scenarios (E1.S3 phase A, `spikes/lang-bench/`)

The workspace templates, the builder, the task-repository fetch and the build box of a workspace are proven on the real thing: the real templates, the real registered snapshots, the real pinned toolchains and the execution layer's own box and chain. The file tests need only `git`; the layer tests need `bwrap`, `systemd-run`, the toolchains and the snapshots, skip with their reason without them, and fail under `BENCH_REQUIRE_SANDBOX=1`. Each test that names a mechanism builds the wrong shape beside the right one. Design: [module_lang_bench.md](module_lang_bench.md), section Workspaces.

```bash
node spikes/lang-bench/run-tests.mjs test/templates.test.mjs test/templates-build.test.mjs test/task-repo.test.mjs test/workspace-diff.test.mjs   # the file tests
BENCH_REQUIRE_SANDBOX=1 LANG_V2_TASK_REPO=<task repository checkout> node spikes/lang-bench/run-tests.mjs 'test/workspace-exec*.test.mjs'    # the layer tests
```

| Flow | Status | Test files |
|---|---|---|
| Each template holds exactly its file set; `build.sh` and `run.sh` hold the contract lines (`run.sh` ends in `exec`, neither sets a variable, mode 755 on disk and in git); the frozen files named in `build.sh` agree with `templates/stack-files.json` | covered | `test/templates.test.mjs` |
| The offline NuGet source is the fixed `%NUGET_PACKAGES%` text; the props file sets no warnings-as-errors policy (the command line does), excludes `tests/` and keeps `Nullable`; `NuGetAudit` is off, none of the ILLink, invariant-globalization or locked-restore properties; `global.json` is the pinned SDK with the Microsoft.Testing.Platform runner; the Node pins; every referenced package is exactly one row of the registered manifest | covered | `test/templates.test.mjs` |
| The three Node locks pin the manifest's versions and integrities, record no install script and list what `package.json` lists; the check reports a planted fault; the build box environment holds no per-case key | covered | `test/templates.test.mjs` |
| Built olympiad workspaces of a stack differ only in statement and samples (no parser, adapter or grader name); the kinds of a stack differ only in the project files; the two stacks differ only in their own files (`diffTaskPair` clean) | covered | `test/templates-build.test.mjs` |
| The builder refuses a destination inside any git worktree (clean, dirty, nested and missing, linked), a linked worktree whose admin folder is gone, a path inside `.git/`, a bare repository, a git that cannot answer, a link, a non-empty folder; builds outside every worktree; a failed write leaves neither the destination nor a staging folder and a retry is not refused | covered | `test/templates-build.test.mjs` |
| The runner in a workspace hashes to the pin; a local runner that is not the pin, a FORMAT that is not the recorded one and a grammar that is not the recorded one are refused naming the hashes; the group rule (a task of another group, a task in no row, an unknown group); each group takes the grammar of its own FORMAT | covered | `test/templates-build.test.mjs` |
| The templates pass the push check's scans (leak, secrets, run-base text, family names, stack-folder file set), name no other stack and no comparison word; a `.cs` or `.ts` file in a task folder stays refused; `stack-files.json` is what the function derives; the command's exit codes (2 usage, including a statement or samples that do not exist, with no stack trace; 3 each refusal, including a cached tree off the hash list; 1 a git failure in the fetch; 0), with a `HOME` of the test's own | covered | `test/templates-build.test.mjs` |
| The task repository fetch: a file off the hash list, a listed file with other bytes, a link under a workspace root, a workspace root that is a link or a regular file; the folder named for the sha without a repository; a changed cache is refused and not fetched again; a non-local repository and a bad sha; no scratch left; the real repository at the pin; the bare copy of one task folder | covered | `test/task-repo.test.mjs` |
| The hello olympiad builds in the build box and the pinned runner and `runCases` (data run) report its verdicts; the daemon and web hello programs are served and stopped by the test's driver; no route off the host; a read-only snapshot unchanged; a hung build killed whole (its sleeps seen first), and a build box that ends when the process that started it is killed with SIGKILL (the same case without the flag leaves the sleeps) | covered | `test/workspace-exec.test.mjs` |
| A package outside the set (`NU1101`, `ENOTCACHED`), a lower version (`NU1603`), a listed package added by the agent, the type check and the lint, the read-only npm cache that looks like a missing package, T-L31 with and without `exec`, the same templates in the agent box with no extra environment (no refused registry request), a stable lock generation; the C# warning policy (a slip is a warning and exit 0, a nullable error and a lower version fail, a higher version fails with `NU1102`), the six project-file overrides against the command line (all six fail with CS8600; an older test with the first-measurement name reads the recorded table), a clean hello of every kind with no warning, a web program with JwtBearer, `tests/` outside the build with `dotnet test --project` and xunit.v3, and the two-build case of an added package with and without `RestoreLockedMode`; F1.S1 (c), added 2026-11-07: a folder named `Bin`, `OBJ` or `Node_Modules` (another letter case) holding text that does not compile is left out of the compile in place and on the scorer's copy made with its own `excludedDirs` (a pin of behaviour that already holds; the test does not import `quickPristineCopy`, because no file of lang-bench imports lang-explore), with a `Bim` folder as its control, which fails the build | covered | `test/workspace-exec-kinds.test.mjs` |
| The Node `.npmrc` of every template holds `offline=true`, `update-notifier=false`, `ignore-scripts=true` and `save-exact=true`, equal to `NPMRC_WORKSPACE`; the `build.sh` comment lines say that the scored copy drops any folder named `bin`, `obj` or `node_modules` in any letter case (C#) and any folder named `node_modules` with `npm ci` making the root one again (Node), and the build lines did not change | covered | `lang-bench/test/templates.test.mjs` |
| T-L62, on the real Node templates in the build box: a `sed` on `tsconfig.json` run as `preinstall`, `install`, `postinstall` or `prepare` leaves the file byte for byte unchanged and TS2322 fails the build; the control without `ignore-scripts` exits 0 with the file rewritten; no lock entry of the three templates (the web lock holds the whole offered set) has an install script; a plain `npm install` of a listed package writes an exact version (the control without `save-exact` writes a range); all three kinds pass `npm ci --offline`, `tsc`, ESLint and `npx --offline`; `npm test` runs its own script; the locks do not move (`--make-locks` says `unchanged`) | covered; the box cases skip with their reason without the toolchains | `lang-bench/test/scripts-stopped.test.mjs` |
| Every function of the workspace files has under 50 lines and a complexity of at most 4; the destination guard is imported by the builder and the task repository and imports neither | covered | `test/code-shape-workspace.test.mjs` |
| A daemon or web verdict reported by the runner; a real task workspace; a real statement | not covered | runner part ii-b; phase B |

**Run numbers** (2026-11-05, final, after `origin/main` was merged (E1.S8, #164, and the epoch-8 plan, D65 to D68), the conventions mount at the merged pin `bd79584`; each run alone, one after the other, 14 CPUs, `/proc/loadavg` at the start and at the end of each run): `BENCH_REQUIRE_SANDBOX=1 LANG_V2_TASK_REPO=… node spikes/lang-bench/run-tests.mjs`: 2266 tests, 2266 pass, 0 fail, 0 skipped, 296 s (load 1.74 to 4.23); this is the story's completion run, with every test passed. Plain `node spikes/lang-bench/run-tests.mjs`: 2261 tests, 2231 pass, 0 fail, 30 skipped (the five network cases, and 25 cases that need the task repository checkout or a real FORMAT, each with its reason), 360 s (load 3.82 to 12.53). `BENCH_REQUIRE_SANDBOX=1 node spikes/minigit-bench/run-tests.mjs`: 1240 tests, 1240 pass, 0 fail, 0 skipped, 449 s (load 7.22 to 4.29). `node --test spikes/lang-explore/test/` (the pilot): 303 tests, 302 pass, 0 fail, 1 skipped (by design), 177 s (load 3.29 to 5.26). `plan-lifecycle.mjs` clean; `push-check.mjs --self --branch feat/lang-v2-e1-s3 --repo .` PASS; `pin-runner.mjs --check`, `pin-exec-layer.mjs --check` and `specs-record.mjs --check` exit 0. `code-shape-lang.test.mjs` (E1.S8) and `code-shape-workspace.test.mjs` both pass after the merge. No test failed in any of the four runs, so nothing is added to S5 of the leftovers plan. The layer tests of this story take about 3 minutes; a hello build in the build box about 3 s per stack and kind; `--make-locks` about 8 s (the locks did not move: the stability test is in the suite).

**What these do not prove.** No daemon or web verdict from the runner (the pinned 0.2.4 judges olympiad only, the driver of the test stands in); no real task, statement or sample (the hello fixture only); one host (WSL2, bwrap, the layer as pinned); the registered snapshots as they are today; the pinned runner is not in the push check's scan (its twelve plan-id lines are a separate story); the Node templates' `.npmrc` turns the npm update check off, so an agent box with no extra environment makes no registry request; a box whose own configuration turns the check back on is not covered; the olympiad kind cannot add `Microsoft.AspNetCore.Authentication.JwtBearer` (a known limit, see the lang-bench module).

## The freeze tools scenarios (F2.S2 (d), `spikes/lang-bench/`)

The freeze CLI (`freeze.mjs`), its guards and the pieces around it are proven on synthetic task repositories: `git init` in owned folders, a bare repository for the GitHub remote (a push is a push into it), a squash merge is `git merge --squash` and a commit on `main`, the specs the conformance fixture's olympiad spec under the six numbers. No test reads a real spec, case, reference or private file, and none touches the network or `gh`. The freeze commit, the tags and the workspace commits are built by plain string edits and git commands in the fixture (`test/support/freeze-fixtures.mjs`), never by the tool under test. Design: [module_lang_bench.md](module_lang_bench.md), section *The freeze tools (F2.S2 (d))*.

```bash
node spikes/lang-bench/run-tests.mjs test/spec-freeze.test.mjs test/spec-freeze-record.test.mjs test/spec-freeze-order.test.mjs test/format-classes-frozen.test.mjs test/workspace-diff.test.mjs test/templates-build.test.mjs
```

| Flow | Status | Test files |
|---|---|---|
| The plan changes only the Freeze tag row of each of the six specs (it applies with `git apply`, one line each); it refuses first, printing nothing, for a Gate verdict that is still its placeholder, a spec gated under FORMAT record A, a conformance check that is not all ok, a FORMAT that is not the step-2a pin, a task list that is not the six, a day that is not today's UTC day, a dirty tree or a HEAD that is not `--ref`, and a FORMAT that fails the class check | covered | `test/spec-freeze.test.mjs` |
| The freeze commit: any other byte of a spec (named by path and line), another path, an unchanged spec, two tags, a row that is not a tag, and any change to FORMAT by one byte are refused; the six rows alone pass | covered | `test/spec-freeze.test.mjs` |
| `specBlobMatches` and `workspaceCommitProblems` (F3.S1's guards): a blob that differs from the tag is refused, a difference only in workspace files is not; given the record, the superseded tag and any tag but `freeze.tag` are refused | covered | `test/spec-freeze.test.mjs` |
| The git sequence under squash merges: tag on C and a workspaces branch cut from C give a W that descends from C; a tag on the freeze branch's head, a W cut before C, a changed spec or FORMAT blob, a merge into main between C and W and a path outside the twelve folders are refused; `--base C` accepts the scratch W and refuses one not cut from C | covered | `test/spec-freeze.test.mjs` |
| `check-local-tag` (another commit, lightweight, changed annotation, already on the remote, name not the checker's pattern, date not the rows', C not on main; a tag that never reached the remote deleted and made again passes) and `check-tag` (a remote lightweight tag, another annotated tag object under a changed message and under the same message with another tagger date, a wrong date, a commit that is not the peeled target, a spec blob that is not the gated one with only its row set, a FORMAT blob that differs from the pin although the freeze commit is self-consistent) | covered | `test/spec-freeze.test.mjs` |
| The hash list covers every file under the four roots plus FORMAT and the six specs, sorted, and `fetchTaskRepo` accepts it; a file added later is refused by the fetch; a modified tracked file and an untracked file in the checkout change nothing (the list is read from W's blobs); a link under a root and a missing commit are refused | covered | `test/spec-freeze.test.mjs` |
| `record` writes the accepted entry and the six rows to one file and nothing else, with the same bytes on a re-run; it refuses a missing or unclean leak-check result, another commit in the evidence, a state other than accepted, an empty W, a W missing a workspace, a workspaces-check file that is missing or ends in another line, a red `check-tag` or `check-workspace`, and a record of another freeze; a record already in the file for the tag is rewritten only with identical bytes (another W, other evidence is refused and the file is untouched); the class check reads the frozen FORMAT from the record it wrote | covered | `test/spec-freeze-record.test.mjs` |
| The superseding tag: a failure after the push leaves the freeze provisional (no record), a later-date tag on a new freeze commit gives `state: "accepted"`, `freeze.tag` the new tag and the first in `superseded`; `--supersedes` needs the old tag on the remote and older | covered | `test/spec-freeze-record.test.mjs` |
| The atomic outputs: a record or a hash list killed (SIGKILL) before its rename leaves the old file whole and only an owned temporary file with its claim; the restart removes a dead owner's file (never reading it), names and leaves one whose owner still runs, and a re-run writes the same bytes | covered | `test/spec-freeze-record.test.mjs` |
| No tag is pushed before the workspaces are proven on the scratch W: a planted wrong byte in one generated workspace, or a needle seen by a fake leak check, stops the sequence with the push seam never called and no tag on the remote; the clean proof reaches the push | covered | `test/spec-freeze-order.test.mjs` |
| The command: the hash list through the process, exit 2 for usage (no verb, an unknown verb, a missing flag, another verb's flag), 3 for a refusal, 4 for a repository git cannot read | covered | `test/spec-freeze.test.mjs` |
| After B is frozen the class check's reference is `freeze.formatSha256` of `spec-states.json`: a common section change is refused and a daemon-web one permitted; A's text is no longer the reference; a states file without the field is exit 4 | covered | `test/format-classes-frozen.test.mjs` |
| `workspace-diff`: the heap sentence and the value of `samples/load.json`'s `heap` key may differ by stack, the key being in both or in neither (`{heap, duration}` against `{duration}` is a finding); any other key, a non-object file and a changed byte in the memory sentence are findings | covered | `test/workspace-diff.test.mjs` |
| `build-workspaces.mjs` refuses 04, 05, 06, 07, 08 and 10 (as `04` or `04-slug`, under either group) and names `explore.mjs workspaces`; `040-x` and other tasks are judged by the older rules | covered | `test/templates-build.test.mjs` |
| The record `freeze.mjs record` writes passes the other half's consumers table (`freezeProblems` over `specStates`), and a record without a leak-check date is named by it | covered | `spikes/lang-explore/test/quick-freeze-record.test.mjs` (red under two mutations of the writer: a result without its date, a workspaces check at a short commit) |
| The freeze's gated hashes come from the registered packages: the default file is `packages.json`, its `specSha256` per task differs from the engine's pin (the frozen text, which equals `spec-states.json`); a package without a 64-hex `specSha256` refuses the whole read naming the task; `explore.json` read as a registry is no source (no fallback); a task with no package has its spec refused; the real freeze commit passes `check-commit` with the default and fails it with the engine's frozen hashes | covered | `test/spec-freeze-pins.test.mjs` (the real-commit case needs `LANG_V2_TASK_REPO`) |
| `PINNED_TASK_SHA` is W: `fetchTaskRepo` at the real task repository verifies against the list of W's 700 files | covered | `test/task-repo.test.mjs` `The_real_task_repository_at_the_pin_matches_the_hash_list` (needs `LANG_V2_TASK_REPO`) |
| The engine's six spec pins equal the freeze record (sha256, path, commit prefix, `draft: false`), and each spec's blob at C hashes to both and the pinned `sha` resolves to C | covered | `lang-explore/test/quick-workspace.test.mjs` (the blob case needs `LANG_V2_TASK_REPO`) |

**Red, green, red again** (2026-11-18, branch `feat/lang-v2-f2s2-freeze-tools`): each file was written first against stubs (or the old code) and failed at its real assertions, then went green, then red again with the implementation put back to the stub or the old file. `spec-freeze.test.mjs`: 30 of 31 (of the 32 it holds today) red on the stubs (the one that passed runs the checker's own `laterForm`; it was red on a missing export before `laterForm` was exported); `spec-freeze-record.test.mjs` 15 of 15 and `spec-freeze-order.test.mjs` 1 of 1 red on the stubs; `format-classes-frozen.test.mjs` 4 new cases red on the old tool; `workspace-diff.test.mjs` 4 new cases red on the old code; `templates-build.test.mjs` 1 red on the old command. 22 single-line mutations of the freeze code (the FORMAT-path rule, the pin comparison, the ancestor and changed-path rules, the remote lightweight and object-id checks, the superseded-tag guard, each `plan` refusal, the record's verifications, the supersede rule, the leak-check commit rule, the restart's judgement of live owners, the link refusal) were each caught by at least one case. Two cases that refuse everything the old code refused (load.json differing in another key) pass on the old code by design; they went red under an over-wide mutation of the fix instead.

**Run numbers** (2026-11-18, `taskset -c 0-5`, at the head with the freeze tools): `node spikes/lang-bench/run-tests.mjs` 2451 tests, 2418 pass, 0 fail, 33 skipped (the cases that need `LANG_V2_TASK_REPO` or the network), 399 s. `BENCH_REQUIRE_SANDBOX=1 node spikes/lang-bench/run-tests.mjs` without `LANG_V2_TASK_REPO`: 2456 tests, 2428 pass, 28 fail, 0 skipped, 459 s; the 28 are the real-draft cases (`The_real_FORMAT_draft_…`, `…_in_the_real_draft_and_the_real_source`, `The_real_task_repository_at_the_pin_matches_the_hash_list`, …), which that flag turns from skips into failures when no task repository is named; the same 28 fail on a detached worktree of `origin/main` (`a7009fd1`), none of them in a file this change touches. The freeze files alone: `spec-freeze` 32, `spec-freeze-record` 15, `spec-freeze-order` 1, all green. No whole run was made with `LANG_V2_TASK_REPO` (the real task repository is not touched here).

**Round 2 fixes, observed (2026-11-18, coai code round, `taskset -c 0-5`).** (a) A direct `buildWorkspace` call for task 04: red `AssertionError [ERR_ASSERTION]: 04 under A` in `A_direct_call_of_buildWorkspace_for_one_of_the_six_tasks_refuses_and_names_the_engine_verb` (the call built the workspace; only the command refused); green after `engineTaskProblem` moved into `preCheck` (`templates-build.test.mjs` 31 of 31; the whole run then found one more case that built 04 under B, `Each_of_the_six_tasks_resolves_to_B_and_each_olympiad_task_to_A`, red `AssertionError: 04 is built under B`, now asserting the engine refusal); red again with the old library, same line. (b) A short write: with a `writeSync` that writes 7 bytes per call, red `AssertionError: all bytes, though each call wrote seven` in `A_writer_that_is_given_short_chunks_loops_until_every_byte_is_written_and_publishes_the_whole_list` (a 7-byte `hashes-lang.txt` was published); with a `writeSync` that reports the whole length and writes 7, red `AssertionError: the command fails` in `A_short_write_that_is_not_reported_is_caught_by_reading_the_temporary_file_back_and_nothing_is_published` (exit 0, a truncated list and, for `record`, a truncated record published); green after the write loop and the read-back (`spec-freeze-record.test.mjs` 18 of 18: the honest case publishes the whole list, the lying case exits non-zero with the old list whole, no temporary file and no record); red again with the old writer, the same two lines. The command parser, `withTag` and the fixtures' bounded git are refactors with no behaviour change, held by the existing tests (`spec-freeze.test.mjs` 32 of 32 before and after).

**With the real task repository** (2026-11-18, a read-only clone at `origin/main` `21a123a`, `LANG_V2_TASK_REPO` set; the tests read FORMAT at the pinned `c204726` with `git show`): `workspace-group-b`, `conformance-record` and `templates-build` 42 of 42, nothing skipped (the group-B cases rewritten for the engine refusal pass against the real record and FORMAT); the whole `BENCH_REQUIRE_SANDBOX=1 LANG_V2_TASK_REPO=… node spikes/lang-bench/run-tests.mjs` 2461 of 2461, 740 s; plain 2456 tests, 2423 pass, 0 fail, 33 skipped, 619 s.

**Round 3 fix, observed (2026-11-18).** A spec edited after its gate: red in `A_spec_edited_after_its_gate_is_refused_by_plan_check_commit_and_check_tag_and_the_pinned_bytes_pass` with `AssertionError [ERR_ASSERTION]: diff --git a/todo/tasks/04-rule-api.md b/todo/tasks/04-rule-api.md …` (`plan` exited 0 and printed a patch for a repository whose spec 06 differs from the engine's pin) and, in `The_pins_are_read_from_the_data_file_and_a_missing_or_unreadable_file_or_task_is_a_refusal`, `Expected values to be strictly equal` (exit 0 where 1 was due: an absent pins file froze the specs anyway); green after `lib/freeze-pins.mjs` (`spec-freeze.test.mjs` 34 of 34: `plan` refuses with exit 3 naming `todo/tasks/06-blog-api.md` and the pins file and prints nothing, `check-commit` and `check-tag` exit 1 naming only that spec, the pinned bytes plan, an absent or task-less pins file refuses); red again with the library put back to the red commit's, the same two failures. The superseding-commit case keeps passing because the row is set back to the placeholder before the hash is taken. `specProblems`, `lineProblem` and `specRows` were split or made immutable with no behaviour change (the same 53 cases of the three freeze files before and after); `record_refuses_a_gate_record_missing_from_the_inputs_naming_each_spec` pins the refusal `specRows` makes.

**Round 4 fixes (the Opus review of PR 4), observed (2026-11-18).** M1, the gated bytes of the six specs, is the pins fix above (a225fbea), covered. Red on the old code, green on the new, red again with the six library files put back; the first line is the red symptom: B1 `AssertionError: the T2 branch head, on no remote ref: record: wrote …/specs.d/spec-states.json` (the unmerged workspaces branch head was recorded), now `record` exits 1 naming "the remote's main" and, for a commit above W, "parent", and the squash W passes; hash list on disk `record: wrote …` with no `hashes-lang.txt`, now exit 1 naming `hashes --write` (absent and stale); evidence `a scratch W″ that is C: record: wrote …` and the dates out of order, now exit 3 (six cases and the control); no ids / a tag named twice `record: wrote …`, now exit 3; acceptedOn `AssertionError: acceptedOn is the date of the clean leak check of W (the accepting step), not the commit's day`, now `2026-12-02`; M3 `AssertionError: no record, no explicit null: the freeze is not accepted` (a guard without `states` passed), now it reads `<inputs>/specs.d/spec-states.json` itself and refuses a superseded tag or no record; the guards with a record `…requires_the_local_tag_to_be_the_recorded_object_and_commit` refuse another tag object and another commit; `check-tag` with no local tag `check-tag ok: the remote's spec-frozen-B-20261201 is annotated, peels to C …` even with `--message-file`, now exit 1 "no local tag"; modes `check-commit ok: the six Freeze tag rows say …` for a spec made executable in the freeze branch, now exit 1 naming the spec and "mode", and the same for a spec or FORMAT mode change in W; the folder fsync `the file is fsynced, renamed, then its folder is fsynced: fsync-file rename`, now `fsync-file rename fsync-dir`. 10 of the 62 cases of `spec-freeze.test.mjs` and `spec-freeze-record.test.mjs` were red on the old code and all 62 are green; with the old libraries back, the same 10 are red. M2 (lang-explore): `an engine file that is on disk and not in HEAD must not pass: workspaces-check ok commit …` (a `.gitignore` matching an engine file, and a wrong byte in HEAD hidden by assume-unchanged with the right bytes on disk, both passed), now the ignored path is refused (exit 4) and the HEAD byte is a finding (`node/05-key-value-server/PACKAGES.md: differs at byte …`); `quick-freeze-workspaces.test.mjs` 15 of 15, red again with the old `workspaces-check.mjs`. Test-only changes: `quick-freeze-record.test.mjs` builds its check file with lang-explore's own `checkLine`; `spec-freeze-order.test.mjs` says in its header that it proves the tools catch a planted fault, not the manual order, and has no fake-needle half.

**Run numbers** (2026-11-19, `taskset -c 0-5`, at the head of that branch): `node spikes/lang-bench/run-tests.mjs` 2599 tests, 2565 pass, 0 fail, 34 skipped (the cases that need `LANG_V2_TASK_REPO` or the network), 713 s. `BENCH_REQUIRE_SANDBOX=1 LANG_V2_TASK_REPO=<the task repository> node spikes/lang-bench/run-tests.mjs` 2604 of 2604, 0 skipped, 597 s. lang-explore plain (`node --test --test-concurrency=1 spikes/lang-explore/test/*.test.mjs`) 1025 tests, 1014 pass, 9 fail, 2 skipped (the two real-repository cases), 483 s. The nine are the same nine on `e3da9ba3` (the branch's base on `main`) in a detached worktree, each by file (`boxes`, `e2e`, `olympiad-rehearse`, `heldout-real`): the host has the `de_DE` locale missing, two SDKs in the box (`the box must hold exactly one SDK (2 listed)`) and no offline .NET packs for the C# reference builds; none touches a freeze file or a pin.

**What these do not prove.** The real task repository's merge-time behaviour, the real specs as gated, GitHub's squash merge and its rulesets, the owner's `gh` login and the real `leakcheck` (they are (e)'s dry run); the engine's workspace verbs (lang-explore's tests); complexity 4 of every function of the new files (the shape rule is not enforced on them).

## The quick run's scenarios (`spikes/lang-explore/`, `spikes/lang-bench/`)

Landing the owner's quick run on `main` (see *The quick run's code on `main`* in [module_lang_bench.md](module_lang_bench.md)). Every fix made while the run was running has a test that was watched going red with the fix
removed (the commands and the failing lines are in the landing brief's `quick-landing-reds.txt`, outside git). Run one file at a time: `node --test spikes/lang-explore/test/<file>`; a file that starts a program, a box or `git` is marked.

**2026-11-06, named failure (D68), head `ba839a7f`, sandbox run, load 3.55 to 4.56, with a diagnostic pinned to CPUs 6 and 7 beside it:** `A_cgroup_that_vanishes_after_child_pid_is_an_environment_failure_and_nothing_is_left` raised `LaunchFailed` where it allowed only `CgroupLost`. The test's own race: the removal can land before or after the runner's presence check at child-pid, as its sibling `..._is_exit_3_on_the_command_line` already says. Both outcomes are no verdict. The test now takes both and checks the sentence.

The previous whole run at `ae7b85e7` had 21 distinct failures, each diagnosed and fixed: a stale `templates/stack-files.json`, three functions over the shape limit and two flags without a table row, tests pinning the old task set or the real explore root, `new Date()` and in-place sorts in new files, and the window tier without the grammar exemption. `rehearse` with the stub CLI passed its 9 criteria on task 05 (daemon) and task 04 (web). `push-check --self` refuses on this branch's history only (one reverted edit, four test-token literals since built at run time); the tree passes as one commit, which is how it lands.

## F1.S2: repeated scoring and the one memory value (`spikes/lang-explore/`, one change in `spikes/minigit-bench/`)

62 flows, each written red first (the commands and the failing test names are in the story's reds file, outside git) and each shown red again with its production line removed. Run one file at a time at low priority:
`node .agents/conventions/tools/low-priority.mjs node --test spikes/lang-explore/test/<file>`. Fakes mean `buildFn`, `runFn`, a fake child, a fake `procApi` and a temp folder; a real-box file needs bwrap, the user bus and the pinned toolchains
(`needs`: skipped with the reason, a failure under `BENCH_REQUIRE_SANDBOX=1`), and imports no fake home because the toolchains are found under the real one.

| C1 to C5: the complete validator of the runner's record | covered | `lang-explore/test/quick-attempt-record.test.mjs` |
| D1 and D2: `verdict.json` rebuilt from the attempt and event files alone, an event is a file written once | covered | `lang-explore/test/quick-verdict-snapshot.test.mjs` |

**Observed (2026-11-07, branch `feat/lang-v2-f1-s2`, one suite at a time at low priority, other sessions sharing the host):** `BENCH_REQUIRE_SANDBOX=1 node --test --test-concurrency=4 spikes/lang-explore/test/` 599 of 599 run after the review round, 0 fail, 1 skipped by design (the 5-million-line leak timing, `LEAK_PERF=1`), 167 s, host load 1.8 at the start; plain: 599 tests, 0 fail, the same 1 skipped, 164 s, load 3.6. The four real-box tests (R1, R2 in both stacks, M7) take about a minute together, with the C# daemon built in the real build box. `BENCH_REQUIRE_SANDBOX=1 node spikes/minigit-bench/run-tests.mjs` 1244 of 1244 before the review round (nothing in minigit-bench changed since); the lang-bench probe files (comments only changed there) 240 pass, 2 skipped.
The first measurement (`research/lang-v2/F1S2_measurement_20261107.md`): ten passes of a session took 298,578 to 310,155 ms with no retry, the retained size of ten passes is 800,952 bytes and of the thirty-attempt worst case 809,061 bytes, the largest built tree 57,888,615 bytes, so a pair's worst case is 117,395,352 bytes.

**What this part does not prove.** Ten passes catch a race that shows often on the scoring CPUs; a rarer one can pass all ten (T25), and the report says so. R1 and R2 run invented daemons laid on the real daemon templates through the real pass child and layer (the C# one is built by the template's `build.sh` in the real build box; the Node one is started as the template's `run.sh` starts it, with no `npm ci`); the web templates are not run by them. The certifying-host check (M7) runs on the owner's host before the freeze and at each
release of the harness, not in CI. The wall bound of the pass child is proved with a fake child that never answers; a real hung layer is not provoked.

## F2.S1: the package tool (`spikes/lang-explore/`)

The package tool's scenarios (F2's stories document, F2.S1 section 2, thirty-seven scenarios and the plan round's five findings) run over INVENTED tasks in temp homes: an invented spec, invented cases, an invented manifest, invented references and witness
overlays, fake template folders and a fake world whose pass seams answer by a plan (`test/quick-package-fixture.mjs`). No private file, no real reference and no key is read. Scenarios 30 to 33 and the grandchild scenario run the REAL box on invented toy daemons laid on the real
templates (`test/quick-package-toys.mjs`), behind `needs` (skipped with the reason, a failure under `BENCH_REQUIRE_SANDBOX=1`).

```bash
node .agents/conventions/tools/low-priority.mjs node --test --test-concurrency=4 spikes/lang-explore/test/quick-package-*.test.mjs
```

| 16 to 20 and S: R-ids, the manifest against the folder, `traps.txt`, the pinned spec hash, F-LOAD's measured rule, the largest reply body; parent entry 10.1: a trap row with a held-out case and a sample the pinned spec holds (green), a sample it does not hold or a row of samples only (red), `traps.txt` the held-out union only, a load case `s01` refused by the manifest shape | `test/quick-package-suite.test.mjs` | the suite checks and `loadRule` |

## F2.S2 (d): the freeze workspaces and the frozen record's consumers (`spikes/lang-explore/`, the lang-explore half of bench PR 4)

The scenarios run over an INVENTED task repository: six invented specs and the synthetic FORMAT of `lang-bench/test/fixtures/conformance/` in a `git init` repository in an owned folder, tagged like a freeze, with the pins' FORMAT hash set to the
fixture's (`test/quick-freeze-fixture.mjs`). The verbs run through `main`; the real templates, runner and `PACKAGES.md` inputs are read, nothing private is. The two interruption families kill a real child process with SIGKILL at a planted
point (`test/quick-freeze-child.mjs`, a seam in `io.workspaces.hooks`), so the owner of what it was making is truly gone, and the restart is the verb run again.

```bash
node .agents/conventions/tools/low-priority.mjs node --test spikes/lang-explore/test/quick-freeze-*.test.mjs
```

| Scenarios | Test file | What it proves |
|---|---|---|
| the written workspace equals what a session gets byte for byte (twelve folders, the manifest, nothing else in the output); a FORMAT that is not the frozen one is refused and writes nothing, and a committed workspace whose grammar is another FORMAT's is a finding naming `runner/GRAMMAR.md` | `test/quick-freeze-workspaces.test.mjs` | `workspaces` over `quickWorkspaceFiles`; T-L57's freeze half |
| the check passes an equal tree with the exact last line and finds one changed byte (the tree hash, then the file and the byte); a missing file, an added file, a lost execute bit; a dirty checkout is refused, not judged; the exported function gives the commit and the findings | `test/quick-freeze-workspaces.test.mjs` | `workspaces --check` and `checkWorkspaces` (F3.S1's `init` calls it) |
| the copy refuses a dirty worktree, a `HEAD` that is not the manifest's commit and a present folder that differs (nothing else copied); a source folder edited after it was made and a manifest that is not the twelve; the writer refuses a destination inside a git worktree, also a linked one | `test/quick-freeze-workspaces.test.mjs` | `workspaces-copy`, `checkDestination` |
| a writer killed before its rename leaves no output folder and only an owned staging folder and its claim, the restart writes it whole and removes them, the bytes equal a second run's; a staging folder whose owner still runs is named and left | `test/quick-freeze-workspaces.test.mjs` | `writeAtomically` with the owner rules, `sweepAndName` |
| a copy killed mid-folder leaves whole folders and one staging folder only, the restart removes it; amendment 2: killed after 3 of 12, the rerun skips the 3 (named) and copies the other 9, a third run copies 0; a present folder that differs after the kill is refused with nothing more copied | `test/quick-freeze-workspaces.test.mjs` | the resumable copy |
| a synthetic frozen record holds every field the pre-registration guard reads (through a stub of F3.S1's guard over `lib/freeze-consumers.mjs`), `freeze.workspacesCheck` included; each field removed is named; a wrong shape, a check that exited 1 or passed at another commit than W is refused; the seal's entry and the memory registration come from the real writers | `test/quick-freeze-consumers.test.mjs` | the table of the consumers' reads (D67 (2)) |

Each scenario was run red first (the verbs unknown to `explore.mjs`, the module absent), green, and red again under a mutation of the code it guards (26 mutations, each red in the test it belongs to).

## F3.S1 (a), bench PR 2: the stage schedule (`spikes/lang-explore/`)

The scenarios of the schedule are pure: each test hands `lib/stage-schedule.mjs` a seed, a run-map built in the test (v1's `openSeries`, `planSession` and `transition`) and the dated read-back lines as objects; nothing is read from disk but `inputs/explore.json`, which one test holds `TASK_KINDS` equal to. No command exists yet (PR 3 adds `stage-run`), so no flow of the CLI catalogue changes.

Every run of these scenarios goes through the mount's wrapper (`common.local-cpu-budget`); the runs of the earlier rounds below were made with `taskset -c 0-5` alone, from code round 3 on every run uses the wrapper.

```bash
node .agents/conventions/tools/low-priority.mjs taskset -c 0-5 node --test spikes/lang-explore/test/stage-schedule.test.mjs spikes/lang-explore/test/code-shape.test.mjs spikes/lang-explore/test/barrier.test.mjs
```

| Scenarios (stories document numbers) | Test (`test/stage-schedule.test.mjs`) | What it proves |
|---|---|---|
| 1, 2 | `Each_round_holds_every_task_once_...`, `The_same_seed_gives_the_same_30_slots_...` | 30 slots numbered in run order, each round every task once, the order inside a round the sha256 order of the seed, the same seed the same slots, twenty seeds more than fifteen different orders |
| 3, 4 | `In_round_r_task_k_runs_C_sharp_on_mask_A_...`, `The_mask_of_a_slot_never_depends_on_the_seed` | all 30 cells against a table written out in the test (C# on A when r + k is odd, Node the other mask); a hundred seeds give one mask table |
| 5, 6 | `Over_the_30_pairs_each_stack_holds_each_mask_exactly_15_times`, `Each_task_gives_a_stack_one_mask_3_times_...` | the parent's balance: 15 and 15 per stack, 3 and 2 per task |
| 7 | `A_swap_per_pair_over_a_seeded_order_can_give_a_task_one_mask_five_times_...` | the refuted approach reproduced (v1's `maskOf` over a seeded order leaves a task on one mask in all five rounds, for a seed the test finds) while the rule gives that task both masks |
| 8 and the first stack | `The_rids_of_a_seed_are_distinct_...`, `The_first_stack_of_a_slot_is_a_seeded_bit_...` | 120 distinct six-hex rids in the parent's form, a seed found offline whose rids collide is refused naming the rid and both sessions; the leading stack varies with the task and the round |
| 9, 10, 11, 12, 13 | `A_slot_is_refused_while_an_earlier_slot_is_not_finished_...`, `No_slot_of_the_next_round_is_offered_...`, `A_void_is_re_run_once_alone_...`, `A_session_with_a_functional_failure_...`, `An_unscored_environment_session_is_a_void_...` | the barrier and the round rule as sentences naming the slot; a halted slot resumed and never planned twice; a void re-run once, alone, under its own rid and the slot's mask, a second void ends the cell; `rejected`, `intermittently incorrect` and `killed: cap` are never re-run, also beside a void of the other stack; an environment void is re-run once |
| 14 and the plan of a slot | `No_data_slot_is_offered_before_the_pilot_is_GO_...`, `A_new_slot_plans_both_stacks_...`, `A_stop_is_never_re_run_...`, `A_data_session_is_seen_through_the_kind_...` | the two gates (the pilot first) and an empty slot list before the reveal; all 30 finished is `ok(null)`; the leading stack launched first with each rid and mask; a stop and a slot holding one stack only are refusals; `series.mjs`'s kind parameter |
| 15, 15a to 15e, 16 | `Round_3_is_refused_...`, `No_slot_after_a_read_back_slot_...`, `A_refuted_read_back_makes_the_session_a_void_...`, `The_re_run_of_a_refuted_session_is_read_back_too_...`, `A_second_refuted_read_back_ends_the_cell_...`, `A_read_back_line_for_a_slot_that_is_no_read_back_slot_...`, `The_read_back_pairs_are_named_by_the_seed_...`, `The_kinds_the_schedule_holds_are_the_kinds_of_the_pins` | the gate (one line of two is not enough, the sentence names the slot and the rids), the refuted line read as a void (also when the run-map already says so), the re-run read back before any later slot, a second refutation ends the cell, lines of another slot, row or verdict word count for nothing, round 3 refused by its own sentence on a hand-made map where the gate was skipped, the pairs one daemon and one web in rounds 1 and 2 from sixty seeds |
| 13a, 15f, 15g, 15h (plan round 2) | `The_offer_names_the_stack_the_attempt_the_rid_and_the_step_...`, `A_scored_attempt_with_a_refuted_line_becomes_its_re_run_through_nextSlot_...`, `A_line_of_the_original_attempt_never_releases_the_re_run_...`, `A_void_attempt_needs_no_line_and_its_re_run_does` | the offer names stack, attempt, rid and step per session state (absent, planned, launching, killed with data, scored, void, re-run planned, one stack only), a restart after the first stack was scored offers only the second and after a void only that stack's re-run; the same map with and without a refuted line goes from the next slot to that attempt's re-run; lines are keyed by the attempt's rid, a void attempt waits for no line |
| 9a, 14a, 15i, 17c, 17d (plan round 2, second pass) | `Omitting_the_kind_leaves_the_quick_runs_pairs_as_they_were_...`, `slotProblem_refuses_what_nextSlot_would_not_offer_...`, `The_first_read_back_line_of_a_rid_is_final_...`, `The_A_A_decision_table_per_state_of_the_twins`, `The_pilot_pairs_follow_the_slot_decision_table` | the kind default and the data kind; `null` for the offered slot in five states and one refusal per invariant (also a slot of `0`, `31`, `1.5`, `'1'`); a later conflicting line refused with both dates (a refutation after a holds, a holds after a refuted), an identical repeat harmless; a row per A/A state (both absent, open twin, one scored and one void runs both again, repeat open, repeat scored, void at attempt 2, one twin only or two `a`, a stop in one twin with the other underway or not) and per pilot-pair state |
| 17b (plan round 2) | `The_pilot_plans_argument_is_validated_field_by_field_...` | the control, 05 and 10 in either order, and one refusal per required field, stack, wrong task and the duplicate, each sentence starting with the field's path |
