## Test leftovers — the C# helper, the start-of-run sweep, the audit, the container pass (E6)

Every test that makes a folder outside its own process keeps the four promises of the leftovers rule: a name from one helper, an owner record, removal by its run, and collection by the next run when this one was killed. The tests of this repository get that from a helper in `tests/Bench.Tests/Leftovers/`. This section says what the helper is, what runs at the start and the end of a run, and what it does not cover.

**The helper.** It is an adapted copy of `dew_flow_mcp` `tests/Mcp.Tests/Leftovers/` at `53f65130` (F42, mcp #81; S2 copied `21553ce`, F42-c re-synced it), with `DirectoryLinks.cs` and `RepositoryRoot.cs`. `template-manifest.json` lists the exact replaces that make the copy fit this repository (the namespace, the token `bench`, the paths, the F29 lock, the start-of-run call). `TheAdaptedCopyTests` undoes them on the raw bytes of each file and compares the git blob SHA, so a copied file cannot change by accident. Never edit a copied file: change the manifest by a reviewed op, in the same commit. `.gitattributes` marks the copied files and the manifest `-text`, so a Windows checkout does not change their line ends. A later story makes a scratch folder with `TempDir.For(what)`: one owned, claimed folder `testsToDelete-<owner>-bench-<what>-<id>`, removed with its claim by `Dispose`. The copied C# guard reads the `container-scoped` marker as the Node guard does (`common/test-leftovers-exemptions.md`), and `LeftoversGuardDifferentialTests` holds the two guards to one scenario table by running the MOUNTED Node guard, which needs Node 20 or later and the mount's `yaml` package; that is why `build-test` installs the mount's node dependencies before the *Test* step.

**The start-of-run sweep.** It runs once per process. A gate (`StartOfRunSweep`) makes the first caller run the sweep and makes every other thread wait for the same result, and an assembly-level attribute makes every test wait on that gate before its body starts. The sweep reads the machine temp root, and it collects only what this user's `bench` owner form names, whose record says the owner is gone, and which is at least 10 minutes old. It lists the rest with a reason (`live`, `unknown`, `foreign`, `legacy`, `unmarked`, `other clone`) and removes nothing else. It never fails the run: an error is one line on stderr. A killed run's git-repository folder with a linked worktree inside is collected whole (case 15).

**The fixture's container and the container pass.** `PostgresFixture` (the real Postgres of the durability tests) refuses to start when the Testcontainers reaper (Ryuk) is switched off: it reads the setting Testcontainers itself acts on (`TestcontainersSettings.ResourceReaperEnabled`: the environment variable, `~/.testcontainers.properties` or code) at construction and again immediately before it builds the container, and says `The Testcontainers reaper (Ryuk) is switched off here …`. Its container has the owner-form name `testsToDelete-<owner>-bench-pg-<id>` from the helper and the label `testsToDelete.owner=<the run's record>`, is never set to be reused (Testcontainers 4.16.0 reads no reuse configuration, observed 2026-11-16; `Reuse_set_through_configuration_alone_stays_off_…` pins that as a green fact, the parent's entry 10.1, and goes red if a dependency bump adds a path), and is built and started only inside `InitializeAsync`, after the container pass. Disposal removes the container and its anonymous volumes; the Docker proofs check that by id and by the volume names captured before. `NewEmptyDatabaseAsync` carries the `container-scoped` marker: the per-test databases live and die with the container. The container pass (`ContainerPass`, benchmark's own file) runs once per process, inside the fixture before its container exists. It lists the containers that carry the owner label, reads them in batches of at most 100, and removes one only on the two keys of the rule: its name and its label are this user's and this repository's, and the label's owner record is `gone`; a container younger than 10 minutes is left alone, and the removal is by the captured id (`rm -f -v <id>`). Every docker call ends within 30 seconds, removals run at most four at a time (with a progress line when there are more than five), a removal that timed out is reported as "outcome unknown" with the count of confirmed removals, an id that vanished between the listing and the read is one notice, every failure is one line of the report (`bench tests: containers — removed <n>, preserved <n>, listed <n>`), and a machine without `docker` gets one notice line. It never fails the run.

**The audit.** After the last test the assembly fixture compares what this process claimed with what is gone. A folder that was never disposed, one that was disposed but not removed, and one that is claimed in the temp root and unknown to the registry fail the run. The first line is `bench tests: this run left <n> folder(s) it claimed — …`, then one line per folder, then a remedy line. An audit that cannot reach the temp root fails the run and says so (`bench tests: the end-of-run audit could not read the temp root …`), keeping what the registry holds, instead of passing as clean (N5). The CI job `build-test` has a step that proves this: `LeftoversSelfTest` leaks a folder on purpose when `BENCH_LEFTOVERS_SELFTEST` is set, and the step is green only when the run fails with that first line and names the folder.

**The fakes and the scratch seams (E6 S5).** The fake CLI finds its script and call log by the MODEL ID the argv pins. An id of the owner form (`<word>-u<uid>-bench-…`, recognised by its segments) names a folder directly under the temp root of the launch, and that folder is the test's own owned folder; any other id keeps `bench-fake-cli/<model>`, and `FAKE_CLI_HOME` still wins. The fake never makes its home: a launch with no `FAKE_CLI_EVENTS` and no home exits 3 at the first write with one stderr line, and `--version` answers without a home. The probe driver (`FakeCli`) makes an `OwnedDir` and uses its own name as the model id, so every subject lives in a folder the sweep can collect. The fake coai has no data folder of its own: `DataDir()` exits 4 with `COAI_DATA_DIR is unset` where the folder is first needed (never at start, so `--version` and `--probe-api` answer), and its test driver (`FakeCoai`) sets `COAI_DATA_DIR` to `<root>/data` inside its owned root, before the test's own variables. The product's scratch worktrees take a root: `ScratchWorktree.DefaultRoot` (`<temp>/bench-scratch`) is the old default, named; `HarvestGates.RunAsync` and `SolutionSignals.RunAsync` have an overload with a trailing `scratchRoot`, and the old signatures delegate with the default, so a benchmark run names its worktrees as before. `DatedGitRepo` keeps its repository (`repo`) and its scratch root (`scratch`) in one owned folder, so a killed test is collected whole, worktree included. Row E6-k's temp paths are removed by the classes that made them: `TempLeftoverChildRunTests` runs each of those classes alone in a child with an owned `TMPDIR` and requires the temp root to be empty afterwards.

**The spikes (E6.S6).** Every test in `spikes/` that makes a folder, a file or a process marker outside its process names it through the SHIPPED Node helper, `ownedFor({ token: 'bench' })` from the mount (`.agents/conventions/tools/lib/owned.mjs`), as `spikes/lang-bench` already did: `ownedDir(t, what)` for a folder the test makes and removes (its `t.after` replaces the `finally` removal), `ownedName(what)` for a marker, a socket or a fixture name, `tempRoot()` for a path only read or never made, `ownedPrefix`, `ownedPattern` and `classifyName` for a test that reads names. No new helper module: a plain `.mjs` is read by no guard. The lang-bench `.py` units make their folders inside the owned `TMPDIR` their Node launcher gives them (`test/support/py-units.mjs`), through two small helpers (`box_support.scratch_dir`, which fails naming `TMPDIR` when it is unset, and `Box.tmp` in `test_run_samples.py`), never a temp API in the lines S6 changed; the older `tempfile.TemporaryDirectory()` calls stay (see the limits). Both guards are clean in `spikes/` but for the listed exemptions: `box_support.py`'s unit name (`legacy-name`: the owner segment is built at run time from `os.getuid()`), `hello/prove.sh`'s `mktemp` (`raw-temp`: a by-hand proof, never a test, removed by its `EXIT` trap), and the two lines of `spikes/lang-bench/runner/run_samples.py` (`:35`, `:306`, C# `raw-temp`) the owner approved as a standing list, which belong to the language-comparison plan (the runner is pinned: any byte of it moves the pin). The minigit runner's start sweep is described with that spike's scenarios above.

**The limits.**

- F29 is closed by copy. On Windows two sweeps of one temp root could both move a folder, so the sweep holds coai's per-root named mutex (`SweepLock.cs`, this repository's own file) over its whole pass. `TwoSweepsAtOnceTests` proves it where the suite runs on Windows. This repository has no Windows CI leg.
- F31 - on Windows, what a run leaves in a process whose NEW files and folders are owned by BUILTIN\Administrators (measured: pwsh on windows-latest) is not removed by the sweep and not named by the audit; it waits for a person. An elevated Git Bash makes the caller's files (measured), and the limit does not apply there. `WindowsNewFileOwnerLimitTests` pins it by reading the owner of a new folder directly; it skips, with its reason, on every other system and where the caller owns the folder.
- On Windows the copied removal clears read-only files below a folder before the delete, never entering a link, and keeps a file with a second hard link (N1, observed by mcp on `windows-latest`, run `43928795620`; not observed here, no Windows leg). Case 15 still skips on Windows, because this repository has no Windows leg to run it on; its skip reason says so.
- The Docker proofs (`FixtureContainerTests`: Ryuk after a SIGKILL, the name and label, the pass on a real daemon, disposal, a reused container, the reuse pin's container half) run only where a daemon answers: under GitHub Actions without one they FAIL, elsewhere they skip with their reason. The pass sees the daemon the `docker` CLI sees, and Testcontainers may be pointed somewhere else (a context, the properties file).
- The reaper check does not read a builder's `.WithCleanUp(false)` (per container); for the fixture's own builder the Ryuk proof is the run-time evidence. The source test `No_cs_file_switches_the_reaper_off` reads every `.cs` file outside the manifest's targets (which the copy check holds to the template's bytes) for the variable's name, whole or assembled from `+`-joined literals, and for an assignment of `false` to the setting; it reads neither a name assembled from identifiers or through `string.Concat` nor a setter reached by reflection.
- What E6 S5 does not prove: Windows (the kill tests skip there); that an owned folder's contents are what a site's comment says (a guard reads the spelling, never the data flow, so a path read through `TempDir.Root` is a statement that nothing creates it, checked by reading its callee); that the product's own temp sites (`FileSystemGateAssessmentFiles`, the solver's patch file, `ScoutTrees`, `GateToolsCommand`) are removed on every path; that a killed fake CLI or fake coai leaves nothing (its `clr-debug-pipe-*` FIFOs and `dotnet-diagnostic-*` sockets stay in the temp root of its launcher); that `ScoutsOutOfProcessTests` and the gate driver's launches work with the fake coai's refusal, which only CI runs (they need Docker).
- **The spikes, what the guards and the sweep do not reach (E6.S6).** (1) No spike suite runs on Windows. (2) A path read through `owned.tempRoot()` or a neutral literal is a statement that nothing creates it; a guard reads spellings, not data flow. (3) A killed run's claimed folders OUTSIDE the temp root are listed, not collected: the fake operator homes the box tests claim under `~` (`agent-box-fs.test.mjs`, `bwrap.test.mjs`: they must lie outside what the box hides), the decoys under `/var/tmp`, and the two sockets under `/run/user/<uid>`; the start sweep reads the temp root and this repository's owner-form git worktrees whose owner is dead, and nothing else (not `~`, not `/var/tmp`, not `/run/user/<uid>`), and the home sweep is `todo/PLAN_bench_test_leftovers_followups.md`'s S1. A whole run lists `~/testsToDelete-*-bench-*`, `/var/tmp/testsToDelete-*-bench-*` and `/run/user/<uid>/testsToDelete-*` by name before and after. (4) Two plain helper modules still make legacy names that no guard reads and that the sweep, with the token `bench`, classes `other` and neither lists nor touches: `spikes/minigit-bench/test/helpers.mjs` (`tmpDir`) and `test/fake-home.mjs` (they wait for the followups plan's F2/S2; `reuse-scan.test.mjs` holds that their creators keep the prefix and that no other module makes one). (5) The C# copy reads only `mkdtemp(` of Python's temp spellings: `tempfile.TemporaryDirectory()`, `tempfile.gettempdir()` and `os.environ["TMPDIR"]` are unread, so that every unit folder is inside the owned `TMPDIR` is the helpers' construction, not a guard's finding. (6) `test/support/py-units.mjs` makes its owned folder when a test file is loaded, which rule §2 asks to be inside a hook or a test; not a guard finding, not changed by S6. (7) The two runner lines above. (8) `tempfile.TemporaryDirectory()` remains in the lang-bench units, older than S6 and not added by it (`test_run_samples.py:54`, `:98`, `:620`; `box_support.py:28`, `:118`; `test_rs_abort.py:22`, `:34`; `test_rs_cgroup.py:21`, `:32`, `:260`; `test_rs_olympiad.py:25`; `test_rs_view.py:12`; `test_rs_launch_mask.py:27`): the C# copy does not read it, and it falls back to the system temp root (`/tmp`) when `TMPDIR` is unset, which only a unit run outside its Node launcher does; the launcher and the `wslc` command give every unit an owned or container-own `TMPDIR`.
- The repository guard test (`LeftoversRepositoryGuardTests`) is copied and stays off (`Explicit`) until story S7 switches it on. Run it by hand with `--explicit only`.

**Commands** (from the repository root; never `dotnet test`):

```bash
# the mount's locked dependencies: the leftovers tests need them (LeftoversGuardDifferentialTests reads workflows through the mount's `yaml`)
npm ci --ignore-scripts --prefix .agents/conventions
T=./tests/Bench.Tests/bin/Release/net10.0/Bench.Tests
node .agents/conventions/tools/low-priority.mjs $T --filter-class "*TheAdaptedCopyTests"
node .agents/conventions/tools/low-priority.mjs $T --filter-class "*LeftoversGuardDifferentialTests"
node .agents/conventions/tools/low-priority.mjs $T --filter-class "*LeftoversSweepCase15Tests"
node .agents/conventions/tools/low-priority.mjs $T --filter-class "*TempDirTests"
node .agents/conventions/tools/low-priority.mjs $T --filter-class "*ContainerPassTests"      # the pass against a fake docker (a Node script): needs node
node .agents/conventions/tools/low-priority.mjs $T --filter-class "*ContainerRulesTests"     # no .cs file switches the reaper off
node .agents/conventions/tools/low-priority.mjs $T --filter-class "*FixtureContainerTests"   # on a machine without Docker the Docker proofs skip with their reason
node .agents/conventions/tools/low-priority.mjs $T --filter-namespace "Bench.Tests.Leftovers"
node .agents/conventions/tools/low-priority.mjs $T --filter-class "*TempLeftoverChildRunTests"   # E6 S5: each class that made temp paths leaves its temp root empty (the Vetting row needs Docker)
node .agents/conventions/tools/low-priority.mjs $T --filter-class "*FakeCliHomeTests"           # E6 S5: the fake CLI's home and the probe driver's owned id
node .agents/conventions/tools/low-priority.mjs $T --filter-class "*FakeCoaiDataFolderTests"    # E6 S5: the fake coai's data folder
node .agents/conventions/tools/low-priority.mjs $T --filter-class "*ScratchRootSeamTests"        # E6 S5: the scratch root seam and its default
node .agents/conventions/tools/low-priority.mjs $T --filter-class "*DatedGitRepoKillTests"       # E6 S5: a killed DatedGitRepo user is collected whole
BENCH_LEFTOVERS_SELFTEST=1 node .agents/conventions/tools/low-priority.mjs $T --filter-class "*LeftoversSelfTest"
node .agents/conventions/tools/low-priority.mjs $T --explicit only --filter-class "*LeftoversRepositoryGuardTests"
```

The self-test run must exit non-zero and print the audit's first line. The guard run is information until S7.

## What the harness does NOT prove

- **That a real claude prints these streams.** The cells replay synthetic streams in claude 2.1.289's shape; the four LIVE streams under
  `tests/Bench.Tests/Fixtures/scouts/` pin the reader's shapes, and E2 S4's pilot is the first real cell.
- **That a real codex judges claims the way the replay does.** The fake answers recorded verdicts keyed by what a row says; §3.7's hand-check
  (≥ 18 of 20 verdicts agreeing with a person opening the file) is what licenses the assessor, and it has not run.
- **That the recall matcher agrees with a person** — §3.7's 19-of-20 hand-check, E2 S5.
- **The read API and the console** for the scouts (E3 S8), and the routes above marked `not covered`.
- **Concurrency of two harnesses on one run**: every scenario runs one campaign at a time, as the design does.
- **The `model-arms` module, whole (E6.S3).** Every verb has its row and its scenario class; the scenarios are POSIX shell shims, a fixture git that is a link to the host's, and the FAKE CLI as `claude` — they skip on Windows. What they prove: the verbs' order, exits, files and trust boundary (what the harness follows, consumes and writes), that nothing the target placed runs for `validate` and `prep-base`'s checks (`ModelArmsArchitectureTests.The_module_launches_nothing_by_a_bare_name` sees a launch a later story adds), the decisions pure over hand-built facts (`ChainPlanTests`, `StepRecoveryTests`, `IsolationPlanTests`), and the detached runners as separate processes (`DetachedLauncherTests`, `ChainProcessTests`). What they never prove: a real claude (the fake replays recorded shapes; the first real step is the owner's), what a real build or suite prints (`prep-base` and `checks` run over RECORDING `dotnet` / `npm` shims — M2 and M3 observed a real tiny solution once), that `checks`' report is TRUE (the arm's code runs with the operator's rights and the report is not authenticated against it, E3 §8 item 17), the gate lock as a real lock except in `FileGateLockTests`, M2's key check and the delegation re-run (M3 step 14) — both outside the suite, run by hand by the owner on 2026-11-04 and logged in `research/module_model_arms.md` (*M2's key check*, *The delegation policy*). M3 ran every verb by hand over the fake (`research/module_model_arms.md`, *M3*) and is not part of the suite: it is `M3PilotScaffold` (`Explicit = true`) plus a hand-run log. macOS and FreeBSD run on the portable road, were never run on a real host (the Mac's road is handed in through the seams, `PlatformKit`), and `run-all`/`chain` refuse there; Windows is built and never run. The product's own bare `git` launches in `src` are outside the structural test. The fixture's `claude`, `dotnet`, `node` and `npm` are empty files outside `PrepBaseTests`.
- **The published record (E6.S3, `PublishedRecordTests`).** It proves no `mcp-coai.json`, `mcp-none.json` or `coai-data` folder sits under `research/local-model-coding/data/`; it does not prove that nothing else secret-shaped is published.
- **The checked-in readers over a `model-arms` arm (E6.S1, `ConsumerCompatibilityTests`).** Seven readers of the harness — `metrics.mjs`, `t-survival.mjs`, `compare.mjs`, `make-tables.mjs`, `metrics6.mjs`, `coai-findings.mjs`, `coai-cost.mjs` — are run from sha256-pinned scratch copies over a fake arm of each scheme (C, CR, CHH, SRS) that `make`, `run-arm` and `checks` wrote against the FAKE CLI, and each is asserted on a number or a name the arm produced, read back from the module's own files; the shell's layout (the seven pinned part-6 scripts) is compared with the module's in both directions, and the one rename (`task-<id>-haiku.*` → `task-<id>-coder.*`) is proved the whole difference for `t-survival.mjs`. It never proves: a real claude's streams (the fake replays recorded shapes); the readers' arithmetic (they are run, not judged), **and the double count it measured** — `metrics.mjs:140` and `compare.mjs:34-35` sum every attempt's `costUSD`, so a resumed gate step reads 3.25 where its session's last result, which `metrics6.mjs` takes, is 2.00 (E2 §8 item 62; the readers are never edited, the analysis plan's port takes the last result per session); **`compare.mjs` over a CR, CHH or SRS arm** — its list (`:12`) holds none of them, so their rows are asserted absent and no arm is ever relabelled to fit; the judges (`ObjectiveReportTests`' round trip stands for them), the fitted unit prices, `survival.mjs`, the mutation checks, and `make-tables.mjs`'s part-6 and part-7 sections (it imports them only when `data/part6` or `data/part7` exists, and the test creates neither) — each excluded script is named with its reason in `PinnedReaders.Excluded`; the files the MODEL writes by brief (`work/tasks.json`, `work/t<n>.md`), which the layout scan cannot see. Linux only (links, `node`, the POSIX shims).
- **Linux and macOS paths for the scouts**: the scenario runs where the suite runs; CI is ubuntu, local runs are Windows.

## Definition of Done for a new flow

A new CLI verb or route ships with its row here in the same task — `covered` naming its scenario test, or `not covered` with a reason.
`ModuleTestsCatalogTests` turns a forgotten row red.

## The language comparison v2 bench (E1.S1, `spikes/lang-bench/`)

A third Node harness: the push check, the run base's tree and `workspace-diff` of the language comparison v2, in `spikes/lang-bench/test/`. Every test
builds throw-away git repositories (a bare `origin` and a task repository with an unrelated history) in owned folders and a synthetic aid; none reads
the private aids, the real secret files or the network. The design is [module_lang_bench.md](module_lang_bench.md), section *Skeleton and the push path*.

```bash
node spikes/lang-bench/run-tests.mjs                                       # everything in spikes/lang-bench/test (lowered, min(8, CPUs - 1) workers)
node spikes/lang-bench/run-tests.mjs 'test/push-*.test.mjs' test/run-base-tree.test.mjs test/workspace-diff.test.mjs   # this story's files
```

| Flow | Status | Test files |
|---|---|---|
| E1.S1: the push check's named checks over a branch, a body text and the bench's own branch (leak, secrets, attribution, run-base text, family names, credits, stack folders, tree shape), exit codes, the report | covered | `test/push-check.test.mjs`, `test/push-cli.test.mjs` |
| E1.S1: the secret shapes, own-value scan, aid shingles, known-task needles, placement rules, credit terms, tree shape, decoding, public texts | covered | `test/push-secrets.test.mjs`, `test/push-needles.test.mjs`, `test/push-visible.test.mjs`, `test/push-credits.test.mjs`, `test/push-shape.test.mjs`, `test/push-decode.test.mjs`, `test/push-public.test.mjs` |
| E1.S1: the range a push adds, `--setup-remote` (idempotent, prune, no tags, LICENSE) | covered | `test/push-range.test.mjs`, `test/push-remote.test.mjs` |
| E1.S1: the run base's tree (cut, credit files, needle scan) and `workspace-diff` | covered | `test/run-base-tree.test.mjs`, `test/workspace-diff.test.mjs` |
| E1.S1: `--tag <name>` (the tag message's body rules; `tag-target` against the remote's CURRENT refs by `ls-remote`, "fetch first" when undecidable) | covered | `test/push-tag.test.mjs` |
| E1.S1: the pre-push hook (`--install-hook`, `--uninstall-hook`, `--force-hook`, the baked literal arguments) | covered | `test/push-hook.test.mjs` |
| E1.S1: the shared bounded process launcher, the chunked batch readers, placement over new (path, blob) pairs | covered | `test/push-launch.test.mjs`, `test/push-scan.test.mjs` |
| E1.S1: credits after an unclosed HTML comment do not count (CodeQL alert 18) | covered | `test/push-credits.test.mjs` |
